```text
349	In such regimes, distributional dimensionality may register contraction before dynamical indicators respond---or, as our experiments show (\S\ref{sec:case_study}), may fail entirely in an architecture-dependent manner.
350	
351	\paragraph{Phase transition theory and the onset boundary.}
352	The onset at $\alpha{\approx}0.50$---quantified empirically as $\alpha_c{=}0.476$ via sigmoid regression (\S\ref{sec:regime})---may reflect a critical mixing threshold analogous to the scaling law of \citet{gu2024cmr} for continual pre-training.
353	The non-monotonic dip at $\alpha{=}0.60$ (\S\ref{sec:regime}) is consistent with the two universality classes of \citet{truong2025entropy}: if the transition is first-order (Class~1, irreversible, exponent $\nu{=}1$), the abrupt simultaneous shift in canary and downstream metrics suppresses the temporal lead that Granger tests exploit, while smoother second-order dynamics (Class~2, reversible, $\nu{=}1/2$) at neighboring $\alpha$ values preserve it.
354	These connections are speculative and require finer $\alpha$ resolution and direct estimation of feedback nonlinearity to validate.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01CKU8K4C4gZZAxRR5rNadQb`</summary>

```text
165	Multi-seed replication across the six validated architecture families would further strengthen the cross-architecture claims; evaluation on larger-scale models (${>}7$B parameters) remains an open direction.
166	Longer training runs ($T{\geq}20$ generations) could unlock threshold onset detection, which requires more generation-over-generation differences for $3\sigma$ calibration to achieve adequate power; longer runs could also improve Toda--Yamamoto's moderate detection rates by providing additional degrees of freedom for the augmented VAR (\S\ref{sec:ty_limitation}).
167	The SmolLM3 finding (ECE-only detection due to non-monotonic entropy) motivates systematic study of which canary metric is most reliable per architecture family.
168	Online monitoring, where canary metrics are computed within training pipelines for real-time collapse detection, is a natural applied extension.
169	Accumulate-mode experiments~\cite{dey2024breaking} would test whether canary dynamics differ from the replace-mode setting studied here.
170	The sigmoid quantification of the onset boundary ($\alpha_c{=}0.476$; \S\ref{sec:regime}) invites connection to theoretical frameworks---the critical mixture ratio scaling law of \citet{gu2024cmr} and the universality classes of \citet{truong2025entropy}---that could explain why canary signals emerge at this contamination regime and whether the non-monotonic dip at $\alpha{=}0.60$ reflects a transition boundary; these connections remain speculative and require finer $\alpha$ resolution to validate.
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Y4LVpkueTgFs2Gajs9w5GV`</summary>

```text
330	We emphasize that the $S \times R$ model provides qualitative intuition, not quantitative derivation; the regime boundaries depend on model capacity, dataset properties, and sample size $N$.
331	
332	\paragraph{Detection threshold vs.\ accumulated contamination.}
333	The detection phase transition at $\alpha_c{=}0.476$ (\S\ref{sec:regime}) reveals that Granger detectability depends on the \emph{per-generation} mixing ratio $\alpha$, not on cumulative contamination.
334	After $G{=}10$ generations, cumulative synthetic fraction reaches $C(\alpha, G) = 1 - (1{-}\alpha)^G$: at $\alpha{=}0.45$, $C = 99.75\%$; at $\alpha{=}0.50$, $C = 99.90\%$---a difference of 0.15 percentage points---yet PermG detection jumps from 0/8 to 6/8.
335	The explanation lies in the per-generation signal.
336	PermG detects temporal lead-lag from the time series of inter-generation entropy shifts $\{\Delta h^{(g)}\}_{g=1}^G$, where $\Delta h^{(g)} \propto \alpha \cdot d_{\mathrm{TV}}(p_g^{\mathrm{model}}, p^{\mathrm{real}})$ captures the per-episode perturbation scaled by the distributional gap at generation~$g$.
337	The critical threshold $\alpha_c$ corresponds to the per-generation signal-to-noise ratio crossing the detection floor: below $\alpha_c$, each episode's signal is too faint for the Granger test to distinguish from noise at $T{=}11$, regardless of how many episodes accumulate.
338	This overturns the implicit assumption in data poisoning research that detection difficulty scales with total contamination; our results show that two training runs with nearly identical cumulative exposure ($99.75\%$ vs.\ $99.90\%$) produce categorically different detection outcomes.
339	
340	\paragraph{Connection to effective rank and detection layers.}
341	The link between entropy and diversity can be made more concrete through effective rank~\cite{roy2007effective}: $\mathrm{erank}(P) = \exp(H(P / \|P\|_1))$ measures the number of dimensions carrying substantial probability mass.
342	A decline in $H$ contracts $\mathrm{erank}(P)$, pushing tail tokens below $\tau_n$ and eliminating the $n$-grams they participate in.
343	This yields a mechanistic chain---$H$ decreases $\to$ $\mathrm{erank}$ contracts $\to$ tail probabilities cross $\tau_n$ $\to$ $D_k^{(g)}$ drops---in which entropy responds first.
344	This chain mediates the temporal resolution factor $R(\alpha)$: at intermediate $\alpha$ (${\approx}0.60$), relaxation time matching compresses the erank-mediated cascade into a single generation ($R \to 0$); at higher $\alpha$, the severity of entropy collapse outpaces the cascade, restoring the generational offset ($R$ large; \S\ref{sec:regime}).
345	
346	Effective rank is a \emph{distributional dimensionality} indicator---it detects contraction of the distribution's effective degrees of freedom---and is conceptually distinct from both distributional instability (variance inflation) and classical critical slowing down (CSD) indicators (rising autocorrelation) that characterize dynamical stability near tipping points~\cite{scheffer2009early,dakos2012methods}.
347	These layers, together with the causal coupling layer captured by Granger-type tests, form four complementary detection channels ordered by onset threshold (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
348	The distinction matters because the layers can diverge: \citet{truong2025entropy} show that entropy collapse can be a first-order phase transition under convex feedback dynamics, in which CSD indicators remain bounded before the critical point and provide no advance warning.
349	In such regimes, distributional dimensionality may register contraction before dynamical indicators respond---or, as our experiments show (\S\ref{sec:case_study}), may fail entirely in an architecture-dependent manner.
350	
351	\paragraph{Phase transition theory and the onset boundary.}
352	The onset at $\alpha{\approx}0.50$---quantified empirically as $\alpha_c{=}0.476$ via sigmoid regression (\S\ref{sec:regime})---may reflect a critical mixing threshold analogous to the scaling law of \citet{gu2024cmr} for continual pre-training, and resonates with the finding of \citet{gu2025datamixing} that data mixing itself can induce phase transitions in knowledge acquisition.
353	The non-monotonic dip at $\alpha{=}0.60$ (\S\ref{sec:regime}) is consistent with the two universality classes of \citet{truong2025entropy}: if the transition is first-order (Class~1, irreversible, exponent $\nu{=}1$), the abrupt simultaneous shift in canary and downstream metrics suppresses the temporal lead that Granger tests exploit, while smoother second-order dynamics (Class~2, reversible, $\nu{=}1/2$) at neighboring $\alpha$ values preserve it.
354	These connections are speculative and require finer $\alpha$ resolution and direct estimation of feedback nonlinearity to validate.
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/docs/paper/method.tex",
  "old_string": "This overturns the implicit assumption in data poisoning research that detection difficulty scales with total contamination; our results show that two training runs with nearly identical cumulative exposure ($99.75\\%$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$) produce categorically different detection outcomes.\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to effective rank and detection layers.}",
  "new_string": "This overturns the implicit assumption in data poisoning research that detection difficulty scales with total contamination; our results show that two training runs with nearly identical cumulative exposure ($99.75\\%$ vs.\\ $99.90\\%$) produce categorically different detection outcomes.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical power interpretation.}\nThe $S \\times R$ framework admits a formal statistical grounding: the detection phase transition at $\\alpha_c$ corresponds to a \\emph{power threshold} rather than a physical phase transition.\nUnder a permutation test framework, detection power scales as $\\mathrm{Power}(\\alpha, T) = \\Phi\\bigl(S(\\alpha) \\cdot R(\\alpha) \\cdot \\sqrt{T} \\,/\\, \\sigma_{\\mathrm{noise}}\\bigr)$, where $\\Phi$ is the standard normal CDF.\nWhen $S(\\alpha) \\cdot R(\\alpha) \\cdot \\sqrt{T}$ first exceeds the detection threshold, power rises sharply from near-zero to near-one, producing the sigmoid-like onset observed empirically.\nThis formalization generates three testable predictions for longer generation chains ($T{=}20$, currently running):\n(i)~$\\alpha_c$ should \\emph{left-shift} (increased $T$ raises power at all $\\alpha$, lowering the detection threshold);\n(ii)~the transition should \\emph{smooth} ($\\sqrt{T}$ scaling broadens the power curve, making onset less step-like); and\n(iii)~different methods should exhibit \\emph{different} $\\alpha_c$ values (each method has its own power curve, so PermG and MMCT weighted consensus may cross the detection threshold at different $\\alpha$).\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to effective rank and detection layers.}"
}
```

     ```latex
     \paragraph{Statistical power interpretation.}
     The $S \times R$ framework admits a formal statistical grounding: the detection phase transition at $\alpha_c$ corresponds to a \emph{power threshold} rather than a physical phase transition.
     Under a permutation test framework, detection power scales as $\mathrm{Power}(\alpha, T) = \Phi\bigl(S(\alpha) \cdot R(\alpha) \cdot \sqrt{T} \,/\, \sigma_{\mathrm{noise}}\bigr)$, where $\Phi$ is the standard normal CDF.
     When $S(\alpha) \cdot R(\alpha) \cdot \sqrt{T}$ first exceeds the detection threshold, power rises sharply from near-zero to near-one, producing the sigmoid-like onset observed empirically.
     This formalization generates three testable predictions for longer generation chains ($T{=}20$, currently running):
     (i)~$\alpha_c$ should \emph{left-shift} (increased $T$ raises power at all $\alpha$, lowering the detection threshold);
     (ii)~the transition should \emph{smooth} ($\sqrt{T}$ scaling broadens the power curve, making onset less step-like); and
     (iii)~different methods should exhibit \emph{different} $\alpha_c$ values (each method has its own power curve, so PermG and MMCT weighted consensus may cross the detection threshold at different $\alpha$).
     ```

```json
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{Model collapse in iterative training.}\n\nThe phenomenon of iterative training collapse was formalized by \\citet{shumailov2024curse}, who showed that recursively replacing real data with model-generated text causes progressive tail erosion across VAEs, GMMs, and language models, and by \\citet{alemohammad2024mad}, who demonstrated quality--diversity tradeoffs in self-consuming generative loops for image models.\nTheoretical analyses have since sharpened these results: \\citet{dohmatob2024tale} recast collapse as a change in scaling-law exponents, showing that even small fractions of synthetic data alter effective scaling behavior, while \\citet{dohmatob2025strong} proved that collapse can occur even when synthetic data constitutes a vanishingly small fraction of the training set.\n\\citet{hataya2023will} provided early empirical evidence that large-scale generative models degrade future datasets through web-scale contamination, showing that even state-of-the-art models produce training-data artifacts detectable in downstream models, a finding that motivates our focus on contamination-level-dependent analysis.\n\\citet{seddik2024bad} formalized the statistical analysis of training on synthetic data, deriving excess risk bounds that quantify how synthetic fraction and model misspecification jointly govern degradation; their framework predicts regime-dependent behavior consistent with the onset boundary we observe empirically at $\\alpha{\\approx}0.45$--$0.50$.\nThe role of data accumulation has been a central theme.\n\\citet{dey2024breaking} proved that accumulating data across generations (rather than replacing it) prevents divergence of population risk in GMMs, HMMs, and language models.\n\\citet{kazdan2025collapse} systematically compared replace and accumulate regimes, finding that synthetic data value depends on the absolute quantity of co-present real data.\n\\citet{fu2025selfconsuming} provided generalization bounds for self-consuming loops, establishing that a constant fraction of real data suffices for convergence under accumulation.\nOn the calibration side, \\citet{he2025golden} showed that optimal real-data weighting converges to the golden-ratio inverse ($1/\\varphi \\approx 0.618$) in Gaussian and GLM settings, and that the risk landscape is non-monotone in mixing ratio, a theoretical prediction with implications for regime-dependent canary behavior.\nBeyond distributional statistics, \\citet{guo2024linguistic} documented progressive decline in lexical, syntactic, and semantic diversity across iterative fine-tuning generations, providing the multi-level diversity metrics that serve as downstream targets in our lead-lag analysis.\n\\citet{schaeffer2025position} catalogued eight distinct and sometimes contradictory definitions of ``model collapse'' in the literature, underscoring the importance of explicit operational definitions, a concern our framework addresses by scoping canary prediction to diversity metrics specifically.\n\n\\paragraph{Canary metrics and collapse monitoring.}\n\nSeveral recent works have proposed monitoring metrics that precede or accompany distributional degradation during iterative training.\n\\citet{du2026geometric} observe that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades in iteratively trained language models, interpreting this through a geometric-regulation lens.\n\\citet{du2025scales} report a similar pattern across model scales and mixing ratios, noting that ECE increases of 75\\% appear at 30\\% synthetic contamination before accuracy drops become visible.\nBeyond calibration, SIGMA~\\cite{sigma2026spectral} introduces a dual-track spectral framework based on embedding Gram matrices, where divergence between deterministic and stochastic spectral bounds serves as an early collapse diagnostic.\n\\citet{kalinowski2026topology} proposes a topological monitoring approach using persistent homology features to detect multi-scale structural loss in representation space.\nDCES~\\cite{dces2026counter} uses center-edge sampling dynamics as a collapse signal and reports improvements in ECE and entropy preservation.\n\\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} introduces surplexity, a surprise-based metric that simultaneously detects collapse and filters training data, demonstrating that high-surplexity selection matches human-data baselines in maintaining distributional balance.\n\nThese works collectively demonstrate growing interest in collapse monitoring, yet they share methodological limitations that weaken their lead-lag claims.\nNone apply differencing tests to distinguish genuine temporal precedence from spurious co-trending; none cross-validate findings across multiple statistical methods; and none apply false discovery rate correction when testing multiple metric pairs.\nOur work differs from the closest predecessor, \\citet{du2026geometric}, in four respects:\n(i)~we compare an entire family of canary metrics rather than a single metric pair;\n(ii)~we cross-validate each lead-lag relationship across five independent statistical methods, quantifying inter-method sensitivity via Cohen's $\\kappa$ and intra-method sensitivity via augmentation order $d_{\\max}$;\n(iii)~we map detectability as a function of contamination regime rather than reporting a single aggregate result; and\n(iv)~we treat method-selection sensitivity itself as a primary finding, showing that it is the dominant source of variation in existing lead-lag conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Training dynamics monitoring.}\n\nA broader literature tracks within-training diagnostics such as loss of plasticity~\\cite{dohare2024plasticity,lyle2023plasticity}, effective representation rank, and gradient norm statistics as indicators of learning health.\nThese approaches monitor a single training run and detect pathologies (saturated units, rank collapse, unstable gradients) that emerge over optimization steps.\nOur work differs in a key structural way: we analyze \\emph{cross-generation} temporal relationships between aggregate statistics computed after each full training cycle, applying time-series causal methods to a sequence of $T{=}10$ generation-level observations rather than tracking step-level dynamics within a single run.\nThis distinction is methodologically consequential because the short, non-stationary series that arise from generational measurement require entirely different statistical tools (Granger causality, permutation tests, differencing diagnostics) than the long, dense traces available from step-level monitoring.\n\n\\paragraph{Early-warning signals and critical transitions.}\n\nThe theoretical foundation for canary-based prediction of system failure originates in ecology and climate science.\n\\citet{scheffer2009early} established that complex systems approaching tipping points exhibit generic early-warning signals (rising autocorrelation and variance due to critical slowing down), independent of system-specific mechanisms.\n\\citet{scheffer2012anticipating} extended this framework by combining empirical warning indicators with network-architecture analysis, arguing that structural and statistical signals together improve anticipatory power.\nCrucially, \\citet{boettiger2012limits} quantified the statistical detection limits of these signals, showing that early-warning power is often negligible in short time series, a constraint directly relevant to iterative training, where the number of generations (typically 5--20) is far smaller than typical ecological datasets.\n\\citet{truong2025entropy} connects entropy collapse to critical transitions, characterizing it as a first-order phase transition with universality classes that determine whether classical early-warning signals retain predictive power.\n\\citet{gu2024cmr} establish a scaling law for predicting critical mixture ratios in continual pre-training, demonstrating that the boundary between beneficial and harmful data mixing follows a power-law relationship with training scale.\n\\citet{gu2025datamixing} show that data mixing itself can induce phase transitions in knowledge acquisition, providing a direct theoretical analogue to the detection phase transition we observe: in both cases, a critical mixing ratio separates qualitatively different regimes rather than producing gradual change.\nThe model collapse literature has not yet imported these methodological tools: existing canary studies neither frame their claims in terms of detection power under finite samples nor test whether observed lead-lag patterns survive the short-series constraints that \\citet{boettiger2012limits} identified as fundamental.\n\n\\paragraph{Lead-lag methodology.}\n\nGranger causality~\\cite{granger1969investigating} provides the standard econometric framework for testing whether one time series improves prediction of another beyond its own history.\n\\citet{shojaie2022granger} review classical and modern extensions, including VAR-based tests, kernel methods, and neural approaches for nonlinear dependencies \\cite{marcinkevics2022neural}.\nA key practical issue is non-stationarity: standard Granger tests applied to integrated (unit-root) series produce inflated rejection rates, the spurious regression problem formalized by \\citet{granger1974spurious}, who demonstrated that regressions between independent random walks yield spuriously significant $t$-statistics, a hazard directly applicable to co-trending metrics in iterative training.\nThe Toda--Yamamoto procedure~\\cite{toda1995statistical} addresses this by augmenting the VAR lag order by the integration order $d_{\\max}$ and testing only the original lags, yielding valid asymptotic inference regardless of cointegration structure; however, this robustness comes at the cost of reduced degrees of freedom, a trade-off that becomes severe in the short series ($T{\\leq}20$) typical of iterative training studies.\nDespite wide use in economics and neuroscience, these methods are largely absent from the machine learning literature on iterative training.\nExisting canary studies rely on visual inspection or naive cross-correlation, which cannot distinguish genuine temporal precedence from shared non-stationary trends.\nOur framework applies five complementary methods (naive cross-correlation, differenced cross-correlation, threshold onset detection, permutation Granger causality, and Toda--Yamamoto Granger causality) and requires multi-method consensus before classifying a lead-lag relationship as robust, directly addressing the method-sensitivity problem that arises when any single test is taken in isolation.\nRecent work on causal method reliability reinforces this multi-method rationale.\n\\citet{ruiz2026causalaudit} propose Causal-Audit, a pre-screening framework that validates causal assumptions before method selection and achieves 62\\% false positive reduction; whereas their approach filters methods \\emph{ex ante}, our consensus framework validates conclusions \\emph{ex post} across methods already applied.\n\\citet{yi2026causalcompass} benchmark causal discovery robustness under eight misspecification scenarios and confirm that no single method dominates universally, directly supporting the multi-method rationale of our consensus framework.\nIndependently, \\citet{sahoo2026calibration} show that calibration metrics are sensitive to training-distribution perturbation beyond the iterative collapse setting, suggesting that ECE-based canary signals may be confounded by non-collapse sources of calibration drift.\n\\citet{falahati2026curated} prove that pluralistic curation of synthetic data can prevent iterative collapse entirely, implying that canary detection is most relevant in uncontrolled data pipelines where such curation is absent.\n\n\\paragraph{Analytical robustness in ML research.}\n\nThe broader issue of researcher degrees of freedom in ML has received increasing attention.\n\\citet{bell2022modeling} introduced multiverse analysis to machine learning, where pipeline choices create a combinatorial space of possible results; \\citet{coretta2023multidimensional} provided direct evidence through a many-analysts design in which 46 independent teams analyzed identical data and reached substantially different conclusions; and \\citet{kapoor2024reforms} synthesized these concerns into REFORMS, a 32-item consensus checklist for ML-based science.\nOur work applies this multiverse perspective to model collapse: five statistical methods across six contamination regimes produce conclusions that range from universal significance to complete null on the same dataset.\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:method-comparison} summarizes the methodological coverage of existing canary and monitoring analyses across the diagnostic dimensions identified in \\S\\ref{sec:pitfalls}.\nNo prior work combines all six dimensions.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Methodological comparison of canary and monitoring analyses in the model collapse literature. \\checkmark\\ = present, $\\times$ = absent, $\\circ$ = partial (${\\leq}2$ levels or limited replication). Diff.\\ = differencing or stationarity controls for spurious regression. Multi-M.\\ = cross-validation across ${\\geq}2$ independent statistical methods. MTC = multiple testing correction (e.g., Bonferroni, BH-FDR). Regime = systematic variation of contamination level~$\\alpha$. Seeds = ${\\geq}3$ independent random seeds. Arch.\\ = validation on ${\\geq}2$ architecturally distinct model families.}\n\\label{tab:method-comparison}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccl}\n\\toprule\nWork & Diff. & Multi-M. & MTC & Regime & Seeds & Arch. & Metrics \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\citet{shumailov2024curse}     & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\circ$    & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & PPL \\\\\n\\citet{du2025scales}           & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & $\\times$   & ECE, Acc, Dist-$n$ \\\\\n\\citet{gambetta2024surplexity} & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & Surplexity, Ent \\\\\nSIGMA~\\cite{sigma2026spectral} & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & Spectral \\\\\nDCES~\\cite{dces2026counter}    & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & $\\times$   & \\checkmark & ECE, Ent, PPL \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Ours}                  & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & \\checkmark & Ent, ECE, PPL, Dist-$n$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}%\n}\n\\end{table}\n\nIn summary, the model collapse literature has established the phenomenon and proposed monitoring metrics, while the econometric literature provides the statistical tools for temporal precedence testing.\nHowever, no prior work bridges these two bodies of literature with the methodological rigor that short, non-stationary time series demand: systematic multi-method cross-validation, differencing diagnostics, false discovery correction, and regime-dependent calibration.\nOur consensus framework fills this gap.",
        "numLines": 102,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 102
      }
    },

  },
```

```text
210	The fitted critical threshold $\alpha_c = 0.476$ with steepness $k \geq 200$ indicates a near-discontinuous onset of causal detectability.
211	Sigmoid $R^2 = 0.969$ vs.\ power-law $R^2 = 0.808$ ($\Delta\text{AIC} = 18.2$).}
212	\label{fig:phase_transition}
213	\end{figure}
214	
215	\paragraph{Non-monotonic dose-response: the synchronous collapse zone.}
216	Dual-seed replication at $\alpha{=}0.60$ reveals a non-monotonic dose-response in Granger detectability: PermG fwd drops to 3/8 (s42) and 0/8 (s43), below both $\alpha{=}0.50$ (6/8, 2~seeds) and $\alpha{=}0.75$ (5--6/8 per seed, 3~seeds).
217	This creates a four-regime structure in the dose-response profile: \emph{blind} ($\alpha{\leq}0.45$, 0/8), \emph{onset} ($\alpha{\approx}0.50$, 6/8), \emph{synchronous dip} ($\alpha{\approx}0.60$, 0--3/8), and \emph{detection} ($\alpha{\geq}0.75$, 5--6/8 per seed; 6/8 at $\alpha{=}1.0$).
218	The dip confirms the non-monotonic prediction of the $S \times R$ framework (\S\ref{sec:theory}): at $\alpha{=}0.60$, signal strength $S$ continues rising but temporal resolution $R$ drops sharply due to relaxation time matching, producing a local minimum in detectability.
219	The synchronous zone is mechanistically distinct from the blind spot: in the blind spot, canary drift is indistinguishable from the null; in the synchronous zone, both canary and downstream metrics drift substantially but without temporal offset.
220	Toda--Yamamoto achieves moderate detection across this range (3--4/8 at $\alpha{\geq}0.50$; \S\ref{sec:ty_limitation}), with its detection at $\alpha{=}0.60$ suggesting partial inter-method complementarity in the synchronous collapse zone where PermG drops to 0--3/8.
221	This non-monotonicity also implies that simple threshold-based triggers (such as the 15\% entropy criterion in \S\ref{sec:intervention}) may outperform formal Granger tests at intermediate contamination levels.
222	
223	\paragraph{Predictions for longer generation chains.}
224	The $S \times R$ framework (\S\ref{sec:theory}) generates testable predictions for longer observation windows ($T{=}20$).
225	Increased $T$ improves temporal resolution without changing signal strength, effectively increasing $R(\alpha)$ by providing more observation points for Granger tests to detect sub-generation lags.
226	We predict: (i)~at $\alpha{=}0.50$, PermG detection should increase from 6/8 to ${\geq}7/8$ as additional generations provide more statistical power for an already-present lead;
227	(ii)~at $\alpha{=}0.60$, the synchronous dip should attenuate or disappear, because finer temporal sampling allows Granger tests to resolve lags that are invisible at $T{=}11$;
228	(iii)~at $\alpha{=}1.0$, detection should remain at 6--8/8, as the strong signal is already saturated.
229	These predictions are submitted for future empirical verification with extended generation protocols.
230	
231	
232	\subsection{Case Study: Canary Prediction of Diversity Collapse}
233	\label{sec:case_study}
234	
235	Having established method sensitivity (Claim~3) and regime dependence (Claim~2), we present the conditions under which canary prediction succeeds (Claim~1).
236	We scope this claim precisely: canary metrics predict \emph{diversity collapse} (declining distinct-$n$), not model collapse broadly.
237	
238	\paragraph{Full synthetic replacement ($\alpha{=}1.0$, 4 seeds).}
239	Token entropy exhibits robust Granger-precedence over diversity collapse metrics, confirmed by 4 independent statistical methods (permutation Granger power${=}1.0$), 4 seeds, and cross-architecture replication on GPT-2-medium (Tier~3 consensus; \S\ref{sec:discussion}).
240	Cross-correlation peaks at lag$={+}1$ with $|r|{>}0.99$ (cross-seed standard deviation: 0.0), establishing a consistent one-generation lead.
241	Figure~\ref{fig:metric_trajectory} illustrates the one-generation offset between token entropy decline and distinct-1 decline, visible across all four seeds.
242	Permutation Granger tests (first-differenced) confirm all canary$\to$diversity pairs as FDR-significant across all four seeds, including distinct-3 (Appendix~\ref{app:distinct3}).
243	Reverse-direction tests (downstream$\to$canary) on WikiText-103 find \textbf{0/24} FDR-significant (all $p{>}0.49$), establishing directional asymmetry in this domain.\footnote{C4 exhibits domain-dependent directionality: at $\alpha{=}0.50$ (s42 fp32), PermG reverse reaches 7/8, indicating near-symmetric bidirectional coupling (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).}
244	
245	\begin{figure}[t]
246	\centering
247	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/metric_trajectory.pdf}
248	\caption{Metric trajectories across 10 generations at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (Pythia-410M, 4~seeds shown as individual traces). Token entropy (top, blue) drops sharply at generation~1, while distinct-1 (bottom, red) begins declining at generations~2--3, establishing a visible one-generation canary lead. The consistent lag across all four seeds confirms that the temporal offset is robust rather than seed-specific. Shaded bands show the inter-seed range. This visual pattern corresponds to the statistical finding of 24/24 forward-direction and 0/24 reverse-direction FDR-significant pairs.}
249	\label{fig:metric_trajectory}
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	When language models train iteratively on their own output, the training distribution progressively loses its tail modes, a phenomenon termed model collapse~\cite{shumailov2024curse,alemohammad2024mad}.
5	Lexical diversity narrows, calibration deteriorates, and each generation inherits the systematic biases of its predecessors.
6	As machine-generated text becomes prevalent on the web, such feedback loops pose a growing and often invisible risk to production training pipelines.
7	By the time standard quality metrics register degradation, the underlying distribution may have already shifted irreversibly.
8	This threat motivates the search for cheap-to-compute \emph{canary metrics}: leading indicators that signal distributional degradation before expensive downstream measures reveal the full extent of collapse.
9	The concept parallels early-warning signals for critical transitions in ecological systems~\cite{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating}, where proxy indicators allow intervention before a tipping point.
10	Several recent studies have pursued this direction: \citet{du2026geometric} observe that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades, SIGMA~\cite{sigma2026spectral} proposes spectral indicators, and DCES~\cite{dces2026counter} uses center-edge sampling dynamics as a collapse signal.
11	
12	Yet the statistical methodology underlying these lead-lag claims has received little scrutiny.
13	Whether a candidate canary genuinely \emph{precedes} downstream degradation, or merely correlates with it under shared non-stationary trends, is a question that existing analyses leave unresolved.
14	Iterative training produces short, noisy time series (often fewer than 15 points per generation sequence), where detection power drops sharply and false positive rates can be high~\cite{boettiger2012limits}.
15	In the absence of proper controls, the choice of statistical method alone can shift conclusions on the same data from universal significance to complete null.
16	This makes the validity of existing canary claims an open question with direct consequences for practitioners who rely on such signals to trigger training interventions.
17	
18	We identify four specific deficiencies that pervade existing lead-lag analyses of iterative training collapse.
19	(1)~No differencing tests rule out spurious regression from shared non-stationary trends, a well-documented hazard in analyses of co-trending time series~\cite{granger1974spurious}.
20	(2)~Conclusions rest on a single statistical method whose assumptions about stationarity and directionality are rarely examined; no multi-method cross-validation is conducted.
21	(3)~No false discovery rate (FDR) correction (e.g., Benjamini--Hochberg) guards against spurious findings when testing many metric pairs simultaneously.
22	(4)~No regime-dependent analysis is performed, despite the possibility that the same test yields opposite conclusions at different contamination levels.
23	The closest predecessor is \citet{du2026geometric}, who report that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades in iteratively trained language models.
24	Their analysis, however, covers a single metric pair, spans only 1--3 generations, and relies on visual comparisons without statistical tests.
25	The early-warning framework of \citet{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating} provides theoretical motivation but has not been applied to training collapse with the rigor that such short, non-stationary series require.
26	
27	We address these gaps through a multiverse-style analysis~\cite{bell2022modeling,kapoor2024reforms} of canary prediction of diversity collapse, the progressive loss of lexical diversity (as measured by distinct-$n$~\cite{li2016diversity}) across training generations.
28	Using Pythia-410M~\cite{biderman2023pythia} across six contamination levels ($\alpha \in \{0.00, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\}$) and up to four random seeds, we apply five statistical methods to expose three methodological pitfalls (method sensitivity, spurious regression, and regime dependence) through the Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT), a formal FPR-inverse-weighted consensus framework inspired by specification curve analysis~\cite{simonsohn2020specification}.
29	An extended eight-point regime map ($\alpha \in \{0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00\}$) reveals a canary blind spot: at $\alpha{=}0.10$, diversity has already collapsed measurably but canary metrics remain at null levels, which places a detection lower bound at $\alpha{\geq}0.50$.
30	Permutation Granger causality emerges as the only method with both zero null-control FPR and high sensitivity; applied to token entropy, it identifies a robust one-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse, and early detection enables timely cessation of synthetic-data training, reducing peak perplexity by 58\% compared to reactive cessation at $\alpha{\geq}0.75$.
31	Cross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium and OPT-1.3B confirms that these findings transfer beyond the primary experimental platform.
32	Granger precedence in our framework establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\cite{shojaie2022granger}; our canary claim is scoped to diversity metrics, not model collapse broadly.
33	To our knowledge, this constitutes the first multiverse-style analysis of lead-lag claims in model collapse: the same dataset, analyzed with five methods across six contamination regimes, yields conclusions that span the full spectrum from universal significance to complete null.
34	
35	Our contributions are:
36	\begin{enumerate}
37	    \item \textbf{Systematic multi-method evaluation framework} (\S\ref{sec:method}, \S\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}).
38	    We apply five lead-lag detection methods to the same data and expose three methodological pitfalls: method choice alone inflates spurious-signal rates to at least 62.5\% (inter-method $\kappa{=}0.185$), first-differencing eliminates canary$\to$perplexity associations entirely ($F{\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$), and regime dependence causes the same Granger test to shift from 20/24 FDR-significant pairs at $\alpha{=}0.75$ to 0/24 at $\alpha{\leq}0.25$.
39	    The Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT) formalizes this comparison through FPR-inverse weighted consensus with null-calibrated $p$-values.
40	    Shapley-value decomposition (exact 32-coalition enumeration; Figure~\ref{fig:shapley}) identifies naive cross-correlation as the sole FPR driver (Shapley(FPR)${=}{+}0.348$) and permutation Granger as the strongest suppressor (Shapley(FPR)${=}{-}0.194$).
41	
42	    \item \textbf{Permutation Granger causality as the recommended detection method} (\S\ref{sec:case_study}, \S\ref{sec:generalization}).
43	    Among the five methods evaluated, permutation Granger causality (PermG) is the only one combining zero null-control FPR with high sensitivity.
44	    PermG identifies token entropy as a one-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse: on WikiText-103, 24/24 forward-direction pairs are FDR-significant and 0/24 in the reverse direction at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (4 seeds $\times$ 6 metric pairs), confirmed across GPT-2-medium, OPT-1.3B, and four additional model families.
45	    Cross-architecture validation across 14 model-seed combinations spanning six families (345M--6.9B) confirms PermG detection (${\geq}6/8$) in 12 cases (86\%).
46	    At $\alpha{=}0.75$, three-seed replication yields 20/24 forward and 0/24 reverse.
47	    This claim is scoped to diversity metrics (distinct-$n$); the perplexity channel is spurious.
48	
49	    \item \textbf{Dose-response characterization revealing a detection phase transition} (\S\ref{sec:regime}).
50	    A signal-times-resolution framework (\S\ref{sec:theory}) predicts non-monotonic detectability; PermG confirms a four-regime structure: 0/8 through $\alpha{=}0.45$ (blind), near-discontinuous onset at $\alpha{=}0.50$ (6/8, dual-seed), a synchronous dip at $\alpha{=}0.60$ (0--3/8), and reliable detection at $\alpha{\geq}0.75$ (5--6/8 per seed, three seeds; 6/8 per seed at $\alpha{=}1.0$, four seeds).
51	    Sigmoid regression quantifies the onset as a detection phase transition ($\alpha_c{=}0.476$, sigmoid $R^2{=}0.969$, $\Delta\alpha{\approx}0.02$), occurring despite only 0.15 percentage points difference in cumulative contamination across the transition boundary.
52	    At $\alpha{=}0.10$, a canary blind spot emerges: diversity has already collapsed (distinct-1 drops 39.6\%) but canary metrics remain indistinguishable from the null condition (entropy drift $-5.3\%$ vs.\ null $-4.6{\pm}0.4\%$, a gap of less than one percentage point).
53	
54	    \item \textbf{Canary-based early detection and cessation} (\S\ref{sec:intervention}).
55	    As a practical demonstration, we compare predictive cessation (triggered when token entropy drops ${>}15\%$) against reactive cessation (triggered when distinct-1 drops ${>}10\%$).
56	    The entropy-based trigger fires one generation earlier, enabling cessation of synthetic-data training before irreversible damage: peak perplexity is reduced by 58\% (pooled estimate; bootstrap 95\% CI: 29--64\%)\footnote{Bootstrap CI computed from two seed-precision pairs (s42 fp32, s43 bf16); the interval reflects observed variation rather than population-level uncertainty.} and convergence completes one generation faster (Generation~2 vs.\ Generation~3).
57	    At $\alpha{=}0.75$, entropy drops 31.6\% at generation~1, exceeding the 15\% threshold and confirming that the trigger generalizes beyond full synthetic replacement.
58	    Results are validated at $\alpha{=}1.0$ across two model scales: Pythia-410M (s42 fp32; s43 bf16, subject to precision effects discussed in \S\ref{sec:discussion}) and Pythia-1.4B (s42 fp32), confirming that the trigger and recovery pattern is scale-independent.
59	\end{enumerate}
60	
61	The remainder of this paper is organized as follows.
62	\S\ref{sec:related_work} surveys related work on model collapse, early-warning signals, and lead-lag methodology.
63	\S\ref{sec:background} establishes the formal setup, notation, and the five detection methods used throughout.
64	\S\ref{sec:method} distills the three methodological pitfalls into a unified diagnostic framework, presents the multi-method consensus pipeline, and reports its empirical calibration.
65	\S\ref{sec:experiments} provides full experimental results: method sensitivity analysis (\S\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}), regime-dependent detectability (\S\ref{sec:regime}), canary prediction of diversity collapse (\S\ref{sec:case_study}), consensus framework validation (\S\ref{sec:consensus_validation}), cross-architecture generalization (\S\ref{sec:generalization}), and canary-based early intervention (\S\ref{sec:intervention}).
66	\S\ref{sec:discussion} discusses implications and future directions, and \S\ref{sec:conclusion} concludes, followed by a dedicated Limitations section (\S\ref{sec:limitations}).
67	
```

consequences for practitioners who rely on such signals to trigger training interventions.\n\nWe identify four specific deficiencies that pervade existing lead-lag analyses of iterative training collapse.\n(1)~No differencing tests rule out spurious regression from shared non-stationary trends, a well-documented hazard in analyses of co-trending time series~\\cite{granger1974spurious}.\n(2)~Conclusions rest on a single statistical method whose assumptions about stationarity and directionality are rarely examined; no multi-method cross-validation is conducted.\n(3)~No false discovery rate (FDR) correction (e.g., Benjamini--Hochberg) guards against spurious findings when testing many metric pairs simultaneously.\n(4)~No regime-dependent analysis is performed, despite the possibility that the same test yields opposite conclusions at different contamination levels.\nThe closest predecessor is \\citet{du2026geometric}, who report that calibration error rises before accuracy degrades in iteratively trained language models.\nTheir analysis, however, covers a single metric pair, spans only 1--3 generations, and relies on visual comparisons without statistical tests.\nThe early-warning framework of \\citet{scheffer2009early,scheffer2012anticipating} provides theoretical motivation but has not been applied to training collapse with the rigor that such short, non-stationary series require.\n\nWe address these gaps through a multiverse-style analysis~\\cite{bell2022modeling,kapoor2024reforms} of canary prediction of diversity collapse, the progressive loss of lexical diversity (as measured by distinct-$n$~\\cite{li2016diversity}) across training generations.\nUsing Pythia-410M~\\cite{biderman2023pythia} across six contamination levels ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00\\}$) and up to four random seeds, we apply five statistical methods to expose three methodological pitfalls (method sensitivity, spurious regression, and regime dependence) through the Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT), a formal FPR-inverse-weighted consensus framework inspired by specification curve analysis~\\cite{simonsohn2020specification}.\nAn extended eight-point regime map ($\\alpha \\in \\{0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00\\}$) reveals a canary blind spot: at $\\alpha{=}0.10$, diversity has already collapsed measurably but canary metrics remain at null levels, which places a detection lower bound at $\\alpha{\\geq}0.50$.\nPermutation Granger causality emerges as the only method with both zero null-control FPR and high sensitivity; applied to token entropy, it identifies a robust one-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse, and early detection enables timely cessation of synthetic-data training, reducing peak perplexity by 58\\% compared to reactive cessation at $\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$.\nCross-architecture validation on GPT-2-medium and OPT-1.3B confirms that these findings transfer beyond the primary experimental platform.\nGranger precedence in our framework establishes temporal predictive improvement, not true causality~\\cite{shojaie2022granger}; our canary claim is scoped to diversity metrics, not model collapse broadly.\nTo our knowledge, this constitutes the first multiverse-style analysis of lead-lag claims in model collapse: the same dataset, analyzed with five methods across six contamination regimes, yields conclusions that span the full spectrum from universal significance to complete null.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n    \\item \\textbf{Systematic multi-method evaluation framework} (\\S\\ref{sec:method}, \\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}).\n    We apply five lead-lag detection methods to the same data and expose three methodological pitfalls: method choice alone inflates spurious-signal rates to at least 62.5\\% (inter-method $\\kappa{=}0.185$), first-differencing eliminates canary$\\to$perplexity associations entirely ($F{\\approx}0$, $p{=}0.99$), and regime dependence causes the same Granger test to shift from 20/24 FDR-significant pairs at $\\alpha{=}0.75$ to 0/24 at $\\alpha{\\leq}0.25$.\n    The Multi-Method Consensus Test (MMCT) formalizes this comparison through FPR-inverse weighted consensus with null-calibrated $p$-values.\n    Shapley-value decomposition (exact 32-coalition enumeration; Figure~\\ref{fig:shapley}) identifies naive cross-correlation as the sole FPR driver (Shapley(FPR)${=}{+}0.348$) and permutation Granger as the strongest suppressor (Shapley(FPR)${=}{-}0.194$).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Permutation Granger causality as the recommended detection method} (\\S\\ref{sec:case_study}, \\S\\ref{sec:generalization}).\n    Among the five methods evaluated, permutation Granger causality (PermG) is the only one combining zero null-control FPR with high sensitivity.\n    PermG identifies token entropy as a one-generation-ahead canary for diversity collapse: on WikiText-103, 24/24 forward-direction pairs are FDR-significant and 0/24 in the reverse direction at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ (4 seeds $\\times$ 6 metric pairs), confirmed across GPT-2-medium, OPT-1.3B, and four additional model families.\n    Cross-architecture validation across 14 model-seed combinations spanning six families (345M--6.9B) confirms PermG detection (${\\geq}6/8$) in 12 cases (86\\%).\n    At $\\alpha{=}0.75$, three-seed replication yields 20/24 forward and 0/24 reverse.\n    This claim is scoped to diversity metrics (distinct-$n$); the perplexity channel is spurious.\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Dose-response characterization revealing a detection phase transition} (\\S\\ref{sec:regime}).\n    A signal-times-resolution framework (\\S\\ref{sec:theory}) predicts non-monotonic detectability; PermG confirms a four-regime structure: 0/8 through $\\alpha{=}0.45$ (blind), near-discontinuous onset at $\\alpha{=}0.50$ (6/8, dual-seed), a synchronous dip at $\\alpha{=}0.60$ (0--3/8), and reliable detection at $\\alpha{\\geq}0.75$ (5--6/8 per seed, three seeds; 6/8 per seed at $\\alpha{=}1.0$, four seeds).\n    Sigmoid regression quantifies the onset as a detection phase transition ($\\alpha_c{=}0.476$, sigmoid $R^2{=}0.969$, $\\Delta\\alpha{\\approx}0.02$), occurring despite only 0.15 percentage points difference in cumulative contamination across the transition boundary.\n    At $\\alpha{=}0.10$, a canary blind spot emerges: diversity has already collapsed (distinct-1 drops 39.6\\%) but canary metrics remain indistinguishable from the null condition (entropy drift $-5.3\\%$ vs.\\ null $-4.6{\\pm}0.4\\%$, a gap of less than one percentage point).\n\n    \\item \\textbf{Canary-based early detection and cessation} (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}).\n    As a practical demonstration, we compare predictive cessation (triggered when token entropy drops ${>}15\\%$) against reactive cessation (triggered when distinct-1 drops ${>}10\\%$).\n    The entropy-based trigger fires one generation earlier, enabling cessation of synthetic-data training before irreversible damage: peak perplexity is reduced by 58\\% (pooled estimate; bootstrap 95\\% CI: 29--64\\%)\\footnote{Bootstrap CI computed from two seed-precision pairs (s42 fp32, s43 bf16); the interval reflects observed variation rather than population-level uncertainty.} and convergence completes one generation faster (Generation~2 vs.\\ Generation~3).\n    At $\\alpha{=}0.75$, entropy drops 31.6\\% at generation~1, exceeding the 15\\% threshold and confirming that the trigger generalizes beyond full synthetic replacement.\n    Results are validated at $\\alpha{=}1.0$ across two model scales: Pythia-410M (s42 fp32; s43 bf16, subject to precision effects discussed in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion}) and Pythia-1.4B (s42 fp32), confirming that the trigger and recovery pattern is scale-independent.\n\\end{enumerate}\n\nThe remainder of this paper is organized as follows.\n\\S\\ref{sec:related_work} surveys related work on model collapse, early-warning signals, and lead-lag methodology.\n\\S\\ref{sec:background} establishes the formal setup, notation, and the five detection methods used throughout.\n\\S\\ref{sec:method} distills the three methodological pitfalls into a unified diagnostic framework, presents the multi-method consensus pipeline, and reports its empirical calibration.\n\\S\\ref{sec:experiments} provides full experimental results: method sensitivity analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:method_sensitivity}), regime-dependent detectability (\\S\\ref{sec:regime}), canary prediction of diversity collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:case_study}), consensus framework validation (\\S\\ref{sec:consensus_validation}), cross-architecture generalization (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}), and canary-based early intervention (\\S\\ref{sec:intervention}).\n\\S\\ref{sec:discussion} discusses implications and future directions, and \\S\\ref{sec:conclusion} concludes, followed by a dedicated Limitations section (\\S\\ref{sec:limitations}).\n",
```json
      "numLines": 67,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 67
    }
  },
```

```text
0	# Paper Framing — distrib_canary_collapse
1	
2	## Target Venue
3	- Primary: **EMNLP 2026 Main Track** (D043, 用户指定)
4	- ARR cycle: June 2026 (deadline ~June 15, 待确认具体日期)
5	- 格式: ACL 系列模板 (acl.sty), 8 页正文 + unlimited appendix
6	- Note: ARR May cycle 已过；June cycle 对齐 EMNLP 2026 commitment deadline
7	
8	## Narrative Structure (D130: Discovery Paper Reframe)
9	
10	**主线**：Detection Phase Transition Discovery。我们发现 canary-downstream 因果检测能力在 α_c≈0.50 附近经历 near-discontinuous onset (sigmoid R²=0.969, k≥200)，且 detection threshold 由 per-generation mixing ratio 决定而非 cumulative contamination。Multi-method 分析是发现这一现象的工具，不是贡献本身。
11	**四根支柱**：(1) Detection phase transition = C1 核心发现；(2) S×R 理论框架 + 5-regime 预测 = C2 机制解释；(3) Multi-method diagnostic toolkit (MMCT) = C3 方法贡献（降级为工具）；(4) Four-layer taxonomy + early cessation + data strategy = C4 实践贡献。
12	**Claim 呈现顺序**：C1 (phase transition discovery) 领衔 → C2 (theoretical framework) → C3 (multi-method toolkit) → C4 (practical applications)。
13	
14	## Core Claims (D130: 4 Contributions)
15	
16	> **Claim mapping (D130)**: D130 重组了贡献结构。旧 Claim 1 (Granger precedence) -> C1/C3; 旧 Claim 2 (regime-dependent) -> C1/C2; 旧 Claim 3 (method sensitivity) -> C3; 旧 Claim 4 (intervention) -> C4。下文 Key Narrative Constraints 中引用 "Claim 1/2/3/4" 仍指旧编号，按此映射解读。
17	
18	**C1: Detection Phase Transition (核心发现)** — Canary-downstream 因果检测能力在 alpha_c approx 0.476 附近经历 steep onset (at T=10; steepness T-dependent per C2 Power Theory)。Sigmoid fit R^2=0.969, steepness k>=200, transition width Delta-alpha approx 0.02。PermG 从 0/8 FDR-sig (alpha<=0.45; sub-threshold signal present: TY=7/8 at α=0.45, fdr_p gradient 0.059–0.094) 跳至 6/8 (alpha=0.50)，cross-seed validated (s42/s43/s44)。**核心 insight**: detection threshold 是 per-generation mixing ratio alpha，NOT cumulative contamination C(alpha,G)=1-(1-alpha)^G。alpha=0.45 cumulative=99.75% vs alpha=0.50=99.90% (0.15pp diff)，但 PermG 0/8 vs 6/8。形式化: Delta-h(g) proportional to alpha * d_TV(p_g^model, p^real)。Cross-architecture: PermG fwd >=6/8 in 10/14 combinations (Pythia-6.9B/1.4B/410M, GPT-2m, OPT-1.3B, Qwen3.5-0.8B, C4-Pythia-1B; exceptions: 1.4B s42=2/8, SmolLM3=3/8)。Four-layer detection taxonomy: (1) effective_rank/dimensionality collapse alpha>=0.10, (2) distributional variance alpha>=0.25, (3) PermG causal coupling alpha>=0.50, (4) AC1 dynamical stability alpha>=0.50 (suggestive, T=11 insufficient)。Onset interval [0.45, 0.50] from 13-point alpha-sweep (alpha=0.25/0.30/0.35/0.40/0.45 all 0/8; alpha=0.25 borderline FDR p=0.053)。**CSD Dissociation (Track A v2, D138)**: Canary CSD 存在 (AR(1) τ=0.79-0.82, variance τ=0.73-0.88, p<0.001), downstream CSD 缺失 (τ=0.03-0.30, p>0.19) → canary-triggered cascade 机制。Canary 二阶渐进失稳 + downstream 一阶突变 → blind zone 内无 lead-lag 可检测。**4-stage cascade**: Structure (α=0.10, eff_rank) → Canary destabilizes (α≥0.25, CSD) → Causal lead-lag emerges (α≥0.50, PermG) → Full cascade (α≥0.75)。CSD 整合入 C1 narrative arc，不独立成 C5（8 页限制）。措辞约束 (D139 更新): "detection phase transition" / "sharp onset" / "steep onset" 允许; "near-discontinuous onset" 降级（a045_s43 TY=7/8 证明信号连续存在）; "first-order phase transition" / "phase transition" (physical sense) 禁止。
19	
20	**C2: Theoretical Framework (机制解释)** — S(alpha) x R(alpha) 竞争模型: Detectability proportional to S(alpha) x R(alpha)。S = signal strength (monotonic increasing with alpha); R = temporal resolution (non-monotonic, drops at alpha approx 0.60 when relaxation time tau_relax(alpha) matches observation interval)。Predicts 4 regimes: (I) Blind zone alpha<0.45 — S too weak; (II) Onset alpha approx 0.45-0.50 — S crosses detection threshold; (III) Sync dip alpha approx 0.55-0.65 — R drops, tau_relax approx Delta-t, zero-lag synchronization destroys PermG lag structure; (IV) Detection plateau alpha>=0.70 — S dominates despite low R。Experimental validation: non-monotonic PermG dose-response 0/8 -> 6/8 -> 0-3/8 -> 5-6/8 matches prediction (dual-seed confirmed at alpha=0.60: s42=3/8, s43=0/8)。Cross-disciplinary connections: hormesis (Calabrese 2003, non-monotonic dose-response), zero-lag GC failure (Deshpande 2010, sync regime), critical speeding up (Titus 2020, near-threshold dynamics)。**D133 Statistical Power Theory (Directive 15)**: S x R 模型有 statistical power 理论基础。alpha_c 不是 physical phase transition 而是 power threshold：Power(alpha, T) = Phi(S(alpha) x R(alpha) x sqrt(T) / sigma)。当 Power 超过检测阈值时产生 "detection phase transition"。**3 个 T=20 可检验预测**：(1) alpha_c LEFT-SHIFT（更多数据 → 更低检测下界）; (2) transition SMOOTHING（power 跨所有 alpha 提升 → 不那么 step-like）; (3) 不同方法有不同 power curve → 不同 alpha_c（PermG vs MMCT）。**a045_s43 TY=7/8 Smoking Gun (D139)**: α=0.45 PermG=0/8 但 TY=7/8 — 因果信号在 α=0.45 已存在（TY 检出 7/8），但 PermG power 不够。直接验证预测(3): method-dependent α_c（TY α_c ∈ [0.30, 0.45], PermG α_c ∈ [0.45, 0.50]）。fdr_p gradient: s43 min=0.059 (距阈值 18%), s42 min=0.091 (距 82%) — power boundary 处 seed 敏感。T=20 实验 RUNNING (t20_a050/a060/a075/a100 + t20_a045 待启) 直接验证预测(1)和(2)。Gu et al. (2025, NeurIPS Spotlight, arXiv:2505.18091) data mixing phase transitions 提供理论类比。
21	
22	**C3: Multi-Method Diagnostic Toolkit (降级为发现工具)** — MMCT (5 methods: naive_fwd + diff_fwd + threshold_onset + PermG + TY) 是发现 C1 的工具，非核心贡献本身。PermG null FPR=0/24 across 3 seeds (唯一 zero-FPR method)。K>=3 consensus 10/14 architecture-seed combinations。Method sensitivity 作为 supporting evidence: naive 100% sig -> differenced PermG 25% sig; 同一 test at alpha=1.0 yields 24/24 FDR-sig vs alpha<=0.45 yields 0/8。三层方法论陷阱: (Layer 1) naive Pearson 一切 "显著"; (Layer 2) 差分翻转整条 perplexity 通道 (8/8 -> 0/8) while diversity preserved (24/24 -> 24/24); (Layer 3) TY d_max sensitivity 0% <-> 94% spurious rate。Evidence from旧 Claims 1-3 仍然有效: Granger-causal precedence at alpha>=0.50 (旧C1), regime-dependent detectability (旧C2), method selection sensitivity (旧C3)。这些在 D130 下从 "core contributions" 降级为 "evidence supporting the discovery"。详细约束见下文 Key Narrative Constraints。
23	
24	**C4: Practical Applications (实践贡献)** — (a) Four-layer detection taxonomy (见 C1): 从业者按 alpha 水平选用不同检测层; (b) Early cessation: 15% token_entropy threshold 触发，比 reactive 早 1 代, ppl reduction 58.3% (bootstrap 95% CI: [28.5%, 63.7%]); Prevention paradox — 有效干预 K>=3=0/8 (gen 1 截断 collapse 轨迹, Granger 丧失统计基础), reactive 8/8 全过; (c) Data strategy warning: replace mode PermG=6/8 vs accumulate mode 0/8 (V-shaped entropy recovery breaks lead-lag), dual-seed robust (s42+s43); (d) bf16 precision sensitivity: gpt2m_s43 bf16=0/8 vs fp32=6/8 — bf16 completely eliminates causal signal。Scope: early cessation confirmed at alpha>=0.75; framing = "detection + timely cessation" not "active recovery"。Prevention paradox insight 是方法论发现的一部分——有效干预在统计上不可检测。
25	
26	## Story Spine (D130: Discovery Paper)
27	
28	- **Problem**: Iterative training on model-generated data causes progressive model collapse, but the fundamental question — **at what contamination level does causal detection become possible?** — remains unanswered. Existing work (Du et al., SIGMA, DCES) uses ad hoc methods without systematic statistical validation across contamination regimes, and no work has characterized how detectability depends on the mixing ratio. Scheffer et al. (2009, 2012) established early-warning indicator frameworks for critical transitions in ecology/climate, but these have not been applied to model collapse.
29	
30	- **Discovery**: We discover a **detection phase transition** — a near-discontinuous onset of causal detectability at alpha_c approx 0.476 (sigmoid R^2=0.969, k>=200, Delta-alpha approx 0.02). Below this threshold, canary-downstream causal coupling is statistically undetectable despite ongoing collapse; above it, PermG reliably detects 6/8 metric pairs. The transition is governed by per-generation mixing ratio alpha, NOT cumulative contamination — 99.75% vs 99.90% cumulative yields 0/8 vs 6/8 detection.
31	
32	- **Mechanism**: We propose the S(alpha) x R(alpha) competition model explaining the non-monotonic detectability pattern. Signal strength S monotonically increases with alpha, but temporal resolution R drops at alpha approx 0.60 when relaxation time matches observation interval. The product S x R predicts 4 regimes (blind -> onset -> sync dip -> detection) validated by PermG dose-response across 13 alpha values and 7+ architectures.
33	
34	- **Tool**: Multi-method diagnostic toolkit (MMCT, 5 independent methods) enables the discovery. PermG achieves 0/24 null FPR. The toolkit reveals systematic method sensitivity: naive correlation finds 100% significant, differenced PermG retains 25%. MMCT is the discovery instrument, not the discovery itself.
35	
36	- **Implications**: Four-layer detection taxonomy for practitioners, early cessation protocol (58.3% ppl reduction), data strategy warning (accumulate mode defeats detection), bf16 precision sensitivity, and prevention paradox.
37	
38	- **Headline result**: "In iterative synthetic training, causal detection of collapse undergoes a near-discontinuous onset at per-generation mixing ratio alpha_c approx 0.50 (sigmoid R^2=0.969). This detection phase transition is explained by competing signal strength and temporal resolution, predicting — and experimentally confirming — a non-monotonic dose-response including a synchronization dip at alpha approx 0.60."
39	
40	- **Title**: "A Detection Phase Transition in Iterative Training Collapse"
41	
42	- **Literature positioning**: Du et al. 是经验前辈但无统计检验框架; Scheffer et al. (2009/2012) 临界转变早期预警框架首次引入 model collapse; Gu et al. (2024, EMNLP, arXiv:2407.17467) data mixing; Gu et al. (2025, NeurIPS Spotlight, arXiv:2505.18091) data mixing phase transitions 提供理论类比; Truong & Truong (2025) universality classes; Calabrese (2003) hormesis 提供非单调 dose-response 跨学科框架; Deshpande (2010) zero-lag GC failure 解释 sync regime; Titus (2020) critical speeding up; Dakos et al. (2012) CSD methods。**核心区别**: 不是提出新方法，而是 **发现新现象** (detection phase transition) + **理论解释** (S x R model) + **实践启示** (taxonomy + early cessation + data strategy)。
43	
44	## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses
45	
46	| Concern | Response Strategy | Required Experiment |
47	|---------|-------------------|---------------------|
48	| "Canary 不预测 perplexity，实用价值有限" | 差分检验已确认 canary→perplexity 为伪回归（D017）。Framing 转为"canary 预测 diversity collapse"——diversity 是 collapse 最早可观测维度，实用价值在于比 perplexity 更早预警。perplexity 通道的伪信号本身是方法论贡献（Claim 3 例证） | 已有数据（D017 确认） |
49	| "ECE 和 token_entropy 对 perplexity 方向相反？" | 差分后两者对 perplexity 均 NOT_SIG（D017），"方向相反"是伪信号的不同表现形式，无需机制性解释。论文诚实报告差分前后对比即可 | 已有数据（D017 确认） |
50	| "只在 Pythia-410M 上验证，scale 泛化？" | Spot-check Pythia-1.4B × 核心条件 | scale-check (P1) |
51	| "WikiText-103 单一数据集" | C4 cross-domain validation | domain-transfer (P1) |
52	| "为什么不用简单相关分析？" | 方法 ablation 展示 5 种方法（naive/差分/threshold/TY/permutation Granger）的结论分歧 + d_max sensitivity 曲线，论证多方法一致性框架的必要性 | method-ablation (P0, 零 GPU) — 已完成 Phase 1 |
53	| "TY Granger 在 N=11 下可靠吗？" | Bootstrap power analysis 证实不可靠（power 0.05-0.20），TY 降级为辅助指标用于 Claim 3 展示。核心分析改用 cross-corr + permutation test + BH FDR | plan_009 (已完成) + plan_006 (P0) |
54	| "Claim 3 说方法不可靠，那 Claim 1 用的方法凭什么可信？" | 多方法一致性 ≠ 单方法可靠性。独立方法有不同偏差方向（naive 偏宽松、TY d_max=1 偏保守），它们在 token_entropy→distinct_1/2 上的收敛本身是证据（类似 meta-analysis 逻辑）。论文 Method/Discussion 需 1-2 段显式论证 | 已有 plan_002 数据 |
55	| "4 个 canary 中 2 个失败，选择偏差？" | 全量报告 4 族结果，诚实讨论 effective_rank/self_bleu 失败原因 | 已有 MVP 数据 |
56	| "Intervention 实验缺失" | 已有 predictive vs reactive 对照实验（s42 fp32 + s43 bf16），Claim 4 升级为 practical demonstration。α=0.75 transitional regime 验证中 | plan_023/024/031/032 已完成, plan_033 RUNNING |
57	| **(D075)** "低污染下 canary 失灵怎么办？" | 诚实报告 α=0.10 盲区（canary 不可区分于 null，但 diversity 已 -39.6%）。定位为检测下界 + diversity 脆弱性的发现。跨 seed + 跨精度稳健（D084） | alpha_sweep_a010 (完成) + a010_s43_fp32 (RUNNING) |
58	| **(D079)** "bf16 vs fp32 结果不一致？" | bf16 stochastic rounding 系统性抑制 collapse（entropy 5.2×, ppl 730×）。主表只用 fp32，bf16 作为"精度对正则化效应"的补充发现。6.9B 因内存限制用 bf16，单独讨论 | D079 约束已落地 |
59	| **(D085)** "为什么 α=1.00 检出率低于 α=0.75？" | 非 BH 伪影，根因是 perplexity 在极端 collapse 下丧失 Granger-testable lag structure（8/8 non-sig 全在 canary→ppl 对）。distinct_N 对 100%。推荐 diversity 类指标作为 downstream | Phase 3 数据 (完成) |
60	| **(新)** "模型都是 2023 年前的，对新架构还成立吗？" | Qwen3.5-0.8B (2026) PermG 8/8 完全通过。SmolLM3-3B (2025) PermG 3/8（仅 ECE pairs 有效，token_entropy 因非单调振荡失效）。跨代泛化成立但 canary 选择影响检测力——丰富 Claim 3 | Qwen 完成, SmolLM3 s43 RUNNING |
61	| "T=10 时间序列太短，TY 统计不可靠" | Bootstrap power analysis 已完成（plan_009）：T=10 power=0.05-0.20, T=20 power=0.17, T=30 power=0.29。TY 降级为 Claim 3 展示，核心用 permutation test | plan_009 (已完成) + plan_006 |
62	| "100+ 假设检验无多重比较校正" | 全 battery 加 Benjamini-Hochberg FDR 校正，报告 adjusted p-values。marginal 结果标注 | 分析代码修改 (P0, 零 GPU) — 已完成 |
63	| "只有 Pythia 一个架构家族" | GPT-2 或 OPT spot-check × 核心条件（至少 1 个非 Pythia 架构） | plan_010 (P1) |
64	| "Incremental over Du et al." | 论文 §Related Work 用 4 点显式区分（多指标/多方法/regime mapping/方法敏感性）。Claim 3 是 Du et al. 完全未涉及的独立贡献 | 已有数据 + framing |
65	| **(D027 新增)** "这只是 negative results paper，没有正面贡献" | 多方法共识框架是正面贡献（explicit framework + sensitivity analysis + FPR/FNR 对比表）；canary prediction（Claim 1 as case study）提供具体可操作的输出（token_entropy 1-gen-ahead canary at α≥0.75）| 已有数据 + 共识框架 formalization（零 GPU）|
66	| **(D027 新增)** "方法论 pitfalls 是 trivial/obvious" | (a) model collapse 文献实际在用 naive 方法（Du et al.）；(b) pitfall 不止于 naive vs formal——differencing × regime 交互是非 trivial 的（差分翻转整条 perplexity 通道但保留 diversity 通道）；(c) d_max sensitivity 曲线证明即使选了 TY，超参数仍致命 | 已有 method_ablation + α-sweep 数据 |
67	| "与 critical slowing down 文献的关系？" | §Related Work 引用 Scheffer et al. 2009/2012 并定位为"将 tipping-point early-warning 框架引入 model collapse"。讨论 rising autocorrelation/variance 类比 | 文献工作 |
68	| "双变量 Granger 无法排除混淆因子" | token_entropy 和 ECE 可能共享信息源（均捕捉 output distribution shape），双变量 Granger 无法排除未观测混淆因子。论文 Discussion 须显式声明 Granger precedence ≠ true causality（Granger 1969），并讨论多变量 VAR 或条件 Granger 作为 future work。多方法一致性（4 种独立方法收敛）部分缓解但不消除此顾虑 | Discussion 声明 + future work |
69	| **(D139)** "Onset 锐度被高估" | TY=7/8 at α=0.45 证明因果信号连续存在，PermG 0/8 是 power threshold 非 signal absence。fdr_p gradient (0.059-0.094) 在 sensitivity analysis 披露。"steep onset" 替代 "near-discontinuous"。Cross-seed raw variance (3/8 vs 6/8) 是 power boundary expected behavior。Skip α=0.47/0.48 (D140): T=20 更有价值 | a045_s43 TY (已有) |
70	| **(D138)** "CSD 证据形式化" | Kendall τ p<0.001, canary 3/4 条件显著。CSD 定位为 C1 cascade mechanism evidence，非独立 claim。4-stage narrative arc 提供 mechanistic explanation | CSD v2 (已完成) |

72	## Venue Fit
73	
74	EMNLP 2026 Main Track 优势：
75	- NLP 社区对 model collapse / data contamination 高度关注，audience 精准
76	- ACL/EMNLP 接受 empirical discovery + analysis paper，不要求 SOTA
77	- Detection phase transition 是新发现（非 incremental methodology contribution）
78	- S(alpha) x R(alpha) 竞争模型提供理论贡献，不只是经验观察
79	- Iterative training / synthetic data 是 LLM 时代热点话题
80	- 跨学科框架 (hormesis, zero-lag GC, CSD) 增加论文深度和 novelty
81	
82	风险：
83	- "phase transition 只是 sigmoid fit" → 13-point alpha-sweep + cross-architecture validation + S x R 理论机制解释，非 curve fitting
84	- 模型规模偏小 (Pythia-410M + GPT-2m) → 诚实标注 limitation + 10/14 cross-architecture + Pythia-6.9B + 1.4B scaling evidence
85	- T=11 短时间序列统计效力 → T=20 验证 RUNNING + TY 降级 + PermG 作为主指标（不依赖 TY）
86	- "只有 2 个 alpha 点支撑 onset" → alpha=0.45 (0/8) + alpha=0.50 (6/8) 是核心，但 alpha=0.25-0.45 五点全 0/8 + alpha=0.25 borderline p=0.053 提供 transition profile
87	
88	> **D130 Claim Mapping**: 下文约束中 "Claim 1" = 旧 Granger precedence (现属 C1/C3), "Claim 2" = 旧 regime-dependent (现属 C1/C2), "Claim 3" = 旧 method sensitivity (现属 C3), "Claim 4" = 旧 intervention (现属 C4)。约束内容仍然有效，只是贡献定位变了。
89	
90	## Key Narrative Constraints (from MVP + plan_003)
91	
92	1. **不能 claim "canary predicts model collapse"** — 只能 claim "canary predicts diversity collapse"。token_entropy→perplexity 经差分检验确认为伪回归（0/4 seed, F≈0, p=0.99, D017），未差分时的"弱信号"完全由共享非平稳趋势驱动。论文措辞用"spurious association confirmed by first-differencing"，不再用"弱信号"
93	2. **ECE→perplexity 和 token_entropy→perplexity 均为伪信号 (D017)** — 差分后两者均 0/4 seed 显著。未差分时观察到的"方向相反"（token_entropy 领先 vs perplexity 领先 ECE）是共享趋势的不同表现形式。论文报告差分前后对比作为方法论教训，不再需要机制性解释
94	3. **effective_rank 和 self_bleu 作为 canary 失败** — 必须全量报告，不能只报正面结果
95	4. **distinct_3 必须放 supplementary，不能完全省略** — max_lag=7 下升为 SIGNIFICANT 但 lag_std>1.5（双峰：seed 42 总是 -5，其余 -1/-2），维持 D008 降级策略，主文聚焦 distinct_1/2。Supplementary 需给完整结果 + 解释 lag instability（高阶 n-gram diversity 对短序列随机变异更敏感）。distinct_3 的不稳定性本身支持 "predictability boundary" 主题——即使 diversity 维度内部也有可预测性梯度
96	5. **max_lag=7 确认核心 lead 无截断** — token_entropy→distinct_1 lag=-1（std=0.0）、ECE→distinct_2 lag=-1（std=0.0）在 max_lag=5/7 下完全一致。token_entropy→perplexity lag=-5 虽非截断伪影，但已确认为非平稳伪回归（D017）
97	6. **~~Dual-timescale 升级条件~~ — 已失效 (D017)** — perplexity 通道经差分确认为伪回归，即使 plan_006 T=20 也无法拯救伪信号。"Two distinct lead horizons" 升级路径关闭。plan_006 的价值重新定位为：(1) diversity lead 在更长序列中的稳定性，(2) TY power 改善观察，(3) 差分后 permutation Granger 在 T=20 下的表现
98	7. **ECE→perplexity "反向因果"已确认为伪信号 (D017)** — 差分后 ECE→perplexity 0/4 SIG。之前的 post-hoc mechanistic hypothesis 无需讨论。论文只需报告差分前后对比
99	8. **TY 在 N=11 下完全不可用于区分真/伪关联** — d_max sensitivity 分析证实：d_max=0 过度宽松（连 effective_rank 都通过），d_max=1 过度保守（连 token_entropy→distinct_1 都拒绝），adaptive 因 ADF 不稳定而跨 seed 震荡。论文**不能**用 TY 作为"正确答案"。**spurious_rate 必须双版本报告**：含 TY 75%（16 pair 中 12 仅 TY 判伪）、排除 TY 后 62.5%（16 pair 中 10 在 4 种非 TY 方法下一致）。ECE→distinct_2/3 被 TY 判伪但在其余 4 种方法全部 4/4 SIG——这本身是"方法选择改变分类"的例证（Claim 3 素材）
100	9. **ECE 地位为 secondary canary — 原因升级为 bidirectionality (D063)** — ECE 前向 consensus 与 token_entropy 等效（PG_fwd 均 6/8），但反向 profile 分离：token_entropy reverse **0/28** across 7 conditions（完美单向因果），ECE reverse **13/28 significant, 5/7 conditions 有 ≥1 reverse pair**，全部为 distinct→ECE 方向。ECE 的 "secondary" 标签不再仅因 TY Type II error，而是基于 **genuine bidirectional coupling evidence**——ECE 与 diversity 存在双向反馈，因果解读受限。论文措辞："While both canaries achieve comparable forward consensus (PG_fwd=6/8), token_entropy shows no reverse significance (0/28), consistent with unidirectional causal precedence; ECE exhibits significant reverse coupling in 5/7 conditions (13/28 pairs), suggesting bidirectional feedback." 这同时服务 Claim 3：单看前向 sig 两者等价，加入反向检验后分离——分析维度选择改变结论
101	10. **d_max sensitivity 曲线是论文的 key figure** — 三组 d_max（0/adaptive/1）× spurious rate 的变化是 Claim 3 的核心可视化，展示"方法选择如何改变结论"
102	11. **统计效力必须显式论证** — Dream 三档一致 CRITICAL：T=10 下 TY 的 DOF 严重不足。论文必须包含 bootstrap/permutation 验证 + plan_006 (T=20) 结果。所有 p-value 必须经 BH FDR 校正
103	12. **"phase diagram" → "regime map"** — 除非 α 分辨率达到 ≥6 个非平凡值 + 提供 α* uncertainty interval，否则用 "predictability regime map" 或 "regime-dependent analysis"。4-5 个 α 值不足以支撑物理学意义上的 "phase diagram"
104	13. **Scheffer et al. 是必引文献** — critical slowing down / early-warning indicators 框架是本研究的知识祖先。论文 Related Work 必须引用并定位
105	14. **CRS 降级为 illustration** — 不列入核心 contribution。CRS 是显然的反应式干预，Dream 三档一致认为 trivial/confounded
106	15. **TY 正式降级为辅助指标 (D015)** — Bootstrap power analysis (N=1000 Monte Carlo): T=10 power=0.05-0.20, T=20 power=0.17, T=30 power=0.29 (d_max≥1)。核心分析框架改为 cross-correlation + permutation test + BH FDR 校正。TY 仅用于 Claim 3 展示（方法敏感性 + power analysis figure），不再作为 lead-lag 判定方法
107	16. **Perplexity 通道确认为伪回归 (D017)** — Permutation Granger 差分版：canary→perplexity 差分后 0/8 SIG (F≈0, p=0.99)，canary→diversity 差分后 12/12 SIG（6 pair × 4 seed 中，token_entropy 和 ECE 各 3 pair 全通过）。差分前后结论翻转（perplexity: 6-8/8→0/8; diversity: 24/24→24/24）是 Claim 3 的关键 figure 素材。**F 值范围**：undifferenced F=74–3370, differenced F=71–7643（全 6 pairs × 4 seeds），范围重叠——之前"F 不降反升"仅对 token_entropy→distinct_1 子集成立，全量数据应以**显著性保持率 24/24→24/24**为核心叙事，不以 F 值大小为论据。论文 Method 节须注明：(1) T=11 first-difference 后 T=10，circular shift 仅 9 个唯一排列，null distribution 高度离散；(2) 尽管如此，diversity 通道 F 值远超任何排列值，结论不受离散性影响
108	17. **f² 效应量报告约束 (D019 更新)** — 差分后 f²=102-435（token_entropy→distinct_1），因 df_resid=5-6 任何真实信号都产生极大 f²（RSS_unrestricted 趋近零）。论文**禁止**用 Cohen 的 0.02/0.15/0.35 基准解读这些 f² 值。正确做法：permutation p-value 为主要推断证据，f² 仅作"效应存在"的定性指示，不做定量比较。**代码层面**：当 df_resid < 20 时，分析脚本输出 `f2_size="df_too_low"` 而非 small/medium/large 分类（D019 T3 已实现于 permutation_granger.py）
109	18. **2×2 差分对比表是 Claim 3 核心 figure** — channel type（canary→diversity vs canary→perplexity）× differencing status（undifferenced vs differenced）的 2×2 表最紧凑展示"一个方法论选择翻转整条通道的结论"。diversity 通道差分前后均 24/24 SIG（显著性完全保持），F 值范围 undiff 74–3370 / diff 71–7643，部分 pair 略降但显著性完全保持。perplexity 通道差分后归零。比 d_max sensitivity 曲线更直观地展示差分的决定性影响
110	19. **method_ablation 必须双版本报告 (D019)** — spurious_rate 报告含 TY（5-method: 75%）和排除 TY（4-method: 62.5%）两个数字。ECE→distinct_2/3 在 TY 下被判伪但在其余 4 种方法中 4/4 SIG——TY 的 Type II error 驱动的分类差异本身是 Claim 3 素材。论文主文用 4-method 数字作主要结论，5-method 数字用于 Claim 3 方法敏感性讨论
111	20. **ECE canary 可靠性是 regime-dependent (D021)** — α=0.25 下 ECE gen 1 下降 -14%（反向信号），gen 2+ 才上升至 +77%。α=1.0 下 gen 1 直接 +2926%。ECE 作为早期预警 canary 仅在高 α（≥0.50，待 a050 确认）下可靠。低 α 下首代反向信号意味着 ECE 不能用于"一代内预警"。这丰富 Claim 2：canary 可靠性本身是 regime-dependent。D019 "co-primary" 限定为 "high-α regime only"
112	21. **Claim 1 scope 确认为 α≥0.75 (D037, Director 批准)** — α=0.75 Permutation Granger FDR: 3/4 方向显著（ECE→d1 p_fdr=0.046, ECE→d2 p_fdr=0.046, entropy→d2 p_fdr=0.048），entropy→d1 边界 p_fdr=0.054。反向 4/4 全部不显著。Consensus 11/16 robust。**当前 Granger-causal precedence 在 α≥0.75 成立**（α=1.0: 24/24 FDR-sig 4 seeds; α=0.75: 3/4 FDR-sig single seed）。**Single-seed caveat (C001)**: α=0.75 仅 seed=42 验证，待 D026 multi-seed 确认后移除限定。**entropy→d1 borderline (p_fdr=0.054)**: 论文须讨论——3/4 显著 + 1/4 边界，不是 4/4 全过。α≤0.50 均 0/8 FDR-sig，detectability boundary 确认在 0.50→0.75 间
113	22. **α=0.50 null 结果是 positive finding (D025, D037 更新)** — 中等污染下 canary 与 downstream 同步退化（temporal lead 消失），correlation/rule-based 方法（naive, differenced, threshold）100% sig vs 因果检验方法（permG, TY）0% sig 是 Claim 3（method × regime interaction）最极端的例证。论文呈现策略：(1) 此非单调 detectability 模式本身是 regime map 的核心特征；(2) **Detectability boundary 已确认在 0.50-0.75 区间 (D037)**——α=0.75 有 3/4 FDR-sig 信号，practical utility 定位为"高污染场景（α≥0.75）下的早期预警"，而非通用预警工具；(3) α=0.50 null 不是方法失败，是 signal-to-noise 的物理边界
114	23. **α-sweep 全部 single seed 是当前最大弱点 (D026)** — 非单调 dose-response（α=0.25 -40.3% vs α=0.50 -37.3%）和 ECE regime switch 假说均基于 seed=42 的 point estimate，无置信区间。论文中非单调性结论**必须标注 "observed at seed=42; multi-seed confirmation required"**。multi-seed（α=0.25/0.50 各 2-3 seeds）是从 Weak Accept → Accept 的首要条件，优先于 finer α grid（a010/a060）。非单调性在主文仅报告现象（配 drift 表格），"entropy floor" 机制假说放 Discussion
115	24. **α=0.25 双向 Granger 不改变 Claim 1 叙事 (D026)** — d1→entropy raw p=0.023（FDR 后 0.093 不显著），但效应量仅 f²=1.30 vs entropy→d1 的 f²=27.21（21 倍差异）。强单向因果 + 弱反馈，不是对称 coupled dynamics。α=1.0 反方向不显著，α=0.50 反方向 p=0.224。论文可加一句："marginally significant feedback at mild contamination (f²=1.30, 21× weaker than primary direction)" 但不需要改写核心叙事
116	25. **证据强度排序：Claim 3 > Claim 2 > Claim 1 (D026)** — Reviewer 独立评估。Claim 3（method sensitivity）最强且 novel、well-supported；Claim 2（regime-dependent）次之（但需 multi-seed）；Claim 1（Granger-causal）scope 最窄（仅 α≥0.75）。论文 framing 应确保 Claim 3 的方法论贡献获得足够篇幅，不因 Claim 1 的 "canary prediction" 叙事而被矮化
117	26. **Phase 3 二次衰退仅限 Discussion，不纳入 Core Claims (D035)** — plan_006_s42 (T=18) 发现 gen 13-17 二次 diversity 衰退（d2 -5.8%, d3 -6.9%），两个硬约束：(a) 单 seed，plan_006_s43 确认前不写入论文主体；(b) Phase 3 中 token_entropy 反升 +2.8% 而 diversity 继续下降——canary 在长 horizon 下失效，升级为核心发现会成审稿攻击面。**最佳用法**：Claim 3 补充证据（观察窗口长度改变结论 = 方法敏感性例证）+ Discussion 段落（collapse dynamics richer than saturation, but canary utility diminishes at extended horizons）。不改变 Core Claims / narrative structure / 方法论主线
118	27. **Consensus score 跨 α 比较须标注标准差异 (D038)** — α=0.75 consensus 11/16 robust 使用单种子 method-agreement 标准（≥4/5 方法判定显著），α=1.0 consensus 6/16 robust 使用 4-seed full consensus 标准（≥3/4 seeds × ≥4/5 方法）。两个数字不可直接对比——11/16 > 6/16 不意味着 α=0.75 信号更强，而是标准更宽松。论文中引用时**必须标注标准差异**，禁止直接对比暗示 α=1.0 信号更弱
```
